<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.
* If the user asks a question, like "why is X happening", don't try to fix the problem. Just give an answer to the question.
</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:
  - Always modify the original file directly when making changes
  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works
  - If you decide a file you created is no longer useful, delete it instead of creating a new version
* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).
</CODE_QUALITY>

<VERSION_CONTROL>

</VERSION_CONTROL>

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes
  - Always use specific keywords that uniquely identify the target process
  - Prefer using `ps aux` to find the exact process ID (PID) first, then kill that specific PID
  - When possible, use more targeted approaches like finding the PID from a pidfile or using application-specific shutdown commands
</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

<issue_description>
Enable Peregrine CLI to use workspace file instead of explicit parameter passing
The Peregrine CLI currently passes simulation parameters via a structured command line call. This should be updated in two ways:

1) Use a workspace file to populate specific parameters for simulation. Once developed, a default workspace file will be distributed within Peregrine (the default file should also be stored in this repo in [cli/peregrine_launcher](https://github.com/ORNL-MDF/Myna/tree/main/cli/peregrine_launcher). Peregrine users will also have an option to specify a custom workspace file. The workspace file path will be passed via command line argument.
2) Implement `argparse` to handle the command line arguments, instead of requiring specific ordering.

Example of the new Peregrine command line call:
```shell
myna launch_peregrine --build server/2024/08/example_build --parts [P5,P21] --layers [1,4,10] --workspace peregrine_default.myna-workspace --mode gv
```
</issue_description>

    22	        "type",
    23	        action="store",

    38	            myna.core.workflow.run.main(parser)

     1	#
     2	# Copyright (c) 2024 Oak Ridge National Laboratory.
     3	#
     4	# This file is part of Myna. For details, see the top-level license
     5	# at https://github.com/ORNL-MDF/Myna/LICENSE.md.
     6	#
     7	# License: 3-clause BSD, see https://opensource.org/licenses/BSD-3-Clause.
     8	#
     9	"""Defines myna launch_peregrine functionality"""
    10	
    11	import sys
    12	import os
    13	import subprocess
    14	import datetime
    15	import contextlib
    16	from pathlib import Path
    17	import yaml
    18	import myna.core.utils
    19	
    20	
    21	@contextlib.contextmanager
    22	def working_directory(path):
    23	    """
    24	    Changes working directory and returns to previous on exit.
    25	
    26	    Reference:
    27	    - https://stackoverflow.com/questions/41742317/how-can-i-change-directory-with-python-pathlib
    28	    """
    29	    prev_cwd = Path.cwd()
    30	    os.chdir(path)
    31	    try:
    32	        yield
    33	    finally:
    34	        os.chdir(prev_cwd)
    35	
    36	
    37	def launch_from_peregrine(argv=sys.argv):
    38	    # Set working directory to the peregrine_launcher interface
    39	    peregrine_launcher_path = os.path.join(
    40	        os.environ["MYNA_INSTALL_PATH"], "cli", "peregrine_launcher"
    41	    )
    42	    with working_directory(peregrine_launcher_path):
    43	        # Parse the arguments passed from Peregrine
    44	        build_path = argv[1]
    45	        layers = [int(x) for x in myna.core.utils.strlist_to_list(argv[2])]
    46	        exported_parts = myna.core.utils.strlist_to_list(argv[3])
    47	        resolution = float(argv[4])
    48	        mode = argv[5].lower()
    49	
    50	        # Write Peregrine input to log file
    51	        lines = []
    52	        lines.append(f"""argv = {argv[0]} {' '.join([f'"{x}"' for x in argv[1:]])}\n""")
    53	        lines.append(f"{build_path=}\n")
    54	        lines.append(f"{layers=}\n")
    55	        lines.append(f"{exported_parts=}\n")
    56	        lines.append(f"{resolution=}\n")
    57	        lines.append(f"{mode=}\n")
    58	
    59	        # Get yyyy-mm-dd_hh-mm format for the current time and add to log file
    60	        now = datetime.datetime.now()
    61	        now_str_pretty = now.strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
    62	        now_str_id = now.strftime("%Y-%m-%d-%Hh-%Mm")
    63	        lines.append(f"\nSimulation started at {now_str_pretty}\n")
    64	
    65	        # Set input file paths
    66	        input_file = f"input_{mode}.yaml"
    67	        output_basedir = "myna_output"
    68	        input_file_configured = os.path.join(
    69	            output_basedir, f"input_{mode}_{now_str_id}.yaml"
    70	        )
    71	        os.makedirs(output_basedir, exist_ok=True)
    72	
    73	        # Get configurable key words
    74	        config_file = "config.yaml"
    75	        with open(config_file, "r") as f:
    76	            config_dict = yaml.safe_load(f)
    77	
    78	        # Set case-specific configurable key words
    79	        config_dict["3DTHESIS_RESOLUTION"] = resolution
    80	        config_dict["MYNA_INPUT_FILE"] = os.path.basename(input_file_configured)
    81	
    82	        # Update key words
    83	        marker = str(config_dict["MARKER"])
    84	        with open(input_file, "r") as f:
    85	            input_lines = f.readlines()
    86	        for i in range(len(input_lines)):
    87	            for key in config_dict.keys():
    88	                if key != "MARKER":
    89	                    old = marker + key + marker
    90	                    new = str(config_dict[key])
    91	                    old_line = input_lines[i]
    92	                    new_line = input_lines[i].replace(old, new)
    93	                    input_lines[i] = new_line
    94	                    if old_line == new_line:
    95	                        print(key)
    96	                        print("\t" + old + " --> " + new)
    97	                        print("\told: " + old_line)
    98	                        print("\tnew: " + new_line)
    99	
   100	        with open(input_file_configured, "w") as f:
   101	            f.writelines(input_lines)
   102	
   103	        # Read and update input dictionary
   104	        output_dir = os.path.basename(build_path).replace(" ", "_") + f"_{now_str_id}"
   105	        with open(input_file_configured, "r") as f:
   106	            input_dict = yaml.safe_load(f)
   107	        input_dict["data"] = {}
   108	        input_dict["data"]["build"] = {}
   109	        input_dict["data"]["build"]["name"] = output_dir
   110	        input_dict["data"]["build"]["path"] = build_path
   111	        input_dict["data"]["build"]["parts"] = {}
   112	        for part in exported_parts:
   113	            input_dict["data"]["build"]["parts"][part] = {"layers": layers}
   114	
   115	        # Export updated input dictionary
   116	        with open(input_file_configured, "w") as f:
   117	            yaml.dump(input_dict, f, default_flow_style=False)
   118	
   119	    # Set working directory to the peregrine_launcher interface output directory
   120	    # to run all myna scripts
   121	    with working_directory(os.path.join(peregrine_launcher_path, output_basedir)):
   122	        # Construct myna_config command
   123	        input_file_configured = os.path.basename(input_file_configured)
   124	        cmd = f"myna_config --input {input_file_configured}"
   125	        p = subprocess.run(
   126	            cmd, shell=True, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT
   127	        )
   128	        out = p.stdout.decode()
   129	
   130	        # Parse output
   131	        lines.append(f"{cmd=}\n\n")
   132	        for line in out.split("\r\n"):
   133	            print(line)
   134	            lines.append(line + "\n")
   135	        lines.append("\n")
   136	
   137	        # Construct myna_run command
   138	        cmd = f"myna_run --input {input_file_configured}"
   139	        p = subprocess.run(
   140	            cmd, shell=True, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT
   141	        )
   142	        out = p.stdout.decode()
   143	
   144	        # Parse output
   145	        lines.append(f"{cmd=}\n\n")
   146	        for line in out.split("\r\n"):
   147	            print(line)
   148	            lines.append(line + "\n")
   149	        lines.append("\n")
   150	
   151	        # Get yyyy-mm-dd_hh-mm format for the current time
   152	        now = datetime.datetime.now()
   153	        now_str_pretty = now.strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
   154	
   155	        # Add time to log file
   156	        lines.append(f"\nSimulation completed at {now_str_pretty}\n")
   157	
   158	        # Write log file
   159	        with open(f"launch_from_peregrine_{now_str_id}.log", "w") as f:
   160	            f.writelines(lines)
   161

     7	
     8	## Description
     9	
    10	Myna is a framework to facilitate modeling and simulation workflows for additive
    11	manufacturing based on build data stored in a digital factory framework, initially
    12	developed for the database structure maintained by ORNL MDF's Peregrine tool.
    13	
    14	There are three top-level submodules within Myna:
    15	
    16	- `core`: contains class definitions for metadata, file types, and workflow components,
    17	as well as the core functionality for running Myna workflows
    18	- `application`: implementations of wrappers for various models to satisfy the
    19	requirements of the defined Myna workflow steps
    20	- `database`: implementations of readers for different database types
    21	
    22	## Installation
    23	
    24	Myna is a Python package which requires Python 3. You can install it locally using `pip install .`,
    25	since the package is not on PyPI. To install `myna` follow the instructions below.
    26	
    27	```bash
    28	# clone repository and change directory to cloned repository
    29	git clone https://github.com/ORNL-MDF/Myna
    30	cd myna
    31	
    32	# To install as a static package
    33	pip install .
    34	
    35	# Or to install as an editable package
    36	pip install -e .
    37	
    38	# Install with all optional dependencies
    39	pip install -e .[dev]
    40	```
    41	
    42	External dependencies are required depending on which applications you wish to use:
    43	
    44	- [3DThesis](https://gitlab.com/JamieStumpORNL/3DThesis)
    45	- [AdditiveFOAM](https://github.com/ORNL/AdditiveFOAM)

     9	import yaml
    10	
    11	
    12	def load_input(filename):
    13	    """Load input file into dictionary
    14	
    15	    Args:
    16	        filename: path to input file (str) to load
    17	
    18	    Returns:
    19	        settings: dictionary of input file settings
    20	    """
    21	
    22	    input_file = filename
    23	    with open(input_file, "r") as f:
    24	        file_type = input_file.split(".")[-1]
    25	        if (file_type.lower() == "yaml") or (file_type.lower() == "myna-workspace"):
    26	            settings = yaml.safe_load(f)
    27	        else:
    28	            print(

    30	            )
    31	
    32	    return settings

1. Current implementation:
   - The CLI entry point is in `/workspace/ORNL-MDF__Myna__1.0/myna/core/workflow/all.py`
   - When `launch_peregrine` is called, it invokes `myna.core.workflow.launch_from_peregrine.launch_from_peregrine()`
   - The actual implementation is in `/workspace/ORNL-MDF__Myna__1.0/myna/core/workflow/launch_from_peregrine.py`
   - Current argument parsing uses positional arguments: `argv[1], argv[2], argv[3], argv[4], argv[5]` for build_path, layers, parts, resolution, mode respectively

2. Workspace file support:
   - The system already supports `.myna-workspace` files through the `load_input` function
   - These are treated as YAML files and can be loaded
   - Components can use workspace files to get configuration parameters
   - There's already an example workspace file at `/workspace/ORNL-MDF__Myna__1.0/examples/example-workspace.yaml`

3. What needs to be implemented:
   - Modify `launch_from_peregrine.py` to use `argparse` instead of manual argument parsing
   - Add support for workspace file loading and parameter extraction
   - Create a default workspace file in `cli/peregrine_launcher/`
   - Update the logic to handle both workspace file parameters and command line overrides

Key files to modify:
1. `/workspace/ORNL-MDF__Myna__1.0/myna/core/workflow/launch_from_peregrine.py` - Main implementation
2. Create `/workspace/ORNL-MDF__Myna__1.0/cli/peregrine_launcher/peregrine_default.myna-workspace` - Default workspace file

def test_current_peregrine_help():
    """Test current help output"""
    print("=== Testing current myna launch_peregrine help ===")
    try:
        result = subprocess.run(['myna', '--help'], capture_output=True, text=True)
        print("STDOUT:", result.stdout)
        print("STDERR:", result.stderr)
        print("Return code:", result.returncode)
    except Exception as e:
        print("Error:", e)

def test_launch_peregrine_current_interface():
    """Test the current interface (should fail gracefully with wrong args)"""
    print("\n=== Testing current launch_peregrine interface ===")
    try:
        result = subprocess.run([
            'myna', 'launch_peregrine',
            '/fake/path', '[1,2]', '[P1]', '20e-6', 'gv'
        ], capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=5)
        print("STDOUT:", result.stdout)
        print("STDERR:", result.stderr)
        print("Return code:", result.returncode)
    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
        print("Process timed out (expected since it tries to do real work)")
    except Exception as e:
        print("Error:", e)

def test_new_peregrine_interface():

    except Exception as e:

            return False

Let me check the original command structure:

Looking at the original readme, the command was:
```bash
myna_peregrine_launcher /home/cloud/myna/resources [51] [P5] 20e-6 gv
```

But now we're calling:
```bash
myna launch_peregrine /fake/build/path [1,2,3] [P1,P2] 20e-6 gv
```

The problem is that `launch_peregrine` is consumed by the `type` argument (which is `nargs="+"`), and all the rest are also consumed by it. So `unknown_args` is empty.

Let me remove the debug prints and fix this properly. I need to change the argument parsing strategy. def main():

import myna


def main():
    """Main function for any myna workflow from the command line"""

    parser.add_argument(
        "type",

Looking at the requirement, I think the best approach is to check if the first element of `args.type` is `launch_peregrine`, and if so, treat the remaining elements as arguments for Peregrine:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/ORNL-MDF__Myna__1.0/myna/core/workflow/all.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>import argparse
import sys

import myna


def main():
    """Main function for any myna workflow from the command line"""

    # Set up argparse
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Setup myna")
    parser.add_argument(
        "type",
        action="store",
        nargs="+",
        help='Run one or more stages of the myna workflow: "config", "run", "sync". Or output the current "status" or "launch_peregrine"',
    )

    # Parse arguments
    args = parser.parse_args()
    
    # Handle launch_peregrine specially - it takes additional arguments
    if "launch_peregrine" in args.type:
        # For launch_peregrine, we need to pass the remaining arguments
        # We reconstruct sys.argv but exclude the 'launch_peregrine' part
        remaining_args = []
        found_launch = False
        for arg in sys.argv[1:]:
            if arg == "launch_peregrine":
                found_launch = True
                continue
            if found_launch:
                remaining_args.append(arg)
        myna.core.workflow.launch_from_peregrine(['myna'] + remaining_args)
    elif "status" in args.type:
        myna.core.workflow.status.write_codebase_status_to_file(parser)
    else:
        # Handle other workflow types normally  
        if "config" in args.type:
            myna.core.workflow.config.main(parser)
        if "run" in args.type:
            myna.core.workflow.run.main(parser)
        if "sync" in args.type:
            myna.core.workflow.sync.main(parser)</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>import argparse
import sys

import myna


def main():
    """Main function for any myna workflow from the command line"""

    # Set up argparse
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Setup myna")
    parser.add_argument(
        "type",
        action="store",
        nargs=1,
        help='Run one or more stages of the myna workflow: "config", "run", "sync". Or output the current "status" or "launch_peregrine"',
    )

    # Only parse known argument (type) since others will only be used per-step.
    args, unknown_args = parser.parse_known_args()
    workflow_type = args.type[0] if isinstance(args.type, list) else args.type
    
    if "status" in workflow_type:
        myna.core.workflow.status.write_codebase_status_to_file(parser)
    elif "launch_peregrine" in workflow_type:
        # Pass the unknown arguments to launch_from_peregrine
        myna.core.workflow.launch_from_peregrine(['myna'] + unknown_args)
    else:

            myna.core.workflow.config.main(parser)

**Problem Statement:**
1. The current Peregrine CLI uses positional arguments that must be passed in a specific order
2. There's no support for workspace files to populate simulation parameters
3. There's no argparse support for named arguments

**Current Behavior:**
- Command: `myna launch_peregrine /fake/build/path [1,2,3] [P1,P2] 20e-6 gv`
- Arguments parsed in order: build_path, layers, parts, resolution, mode
- No workspace file support
- No named arguments support

**Requirements:**
1. Enable workspace file support to populate parameters
2. Distribute a default workspace file in cli/peregrine_launcher
3. Allow custom workspace file via command line
4. Implement argparse for named arguments instead of positional ordering
5. New command format: `myna launch_peregrine --build server/2024/08/example_build --parts [P5,P21] --layers [1,4,10] --workspace peregrine_default.myna-workspace --mode gv`

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now I'll rewrite the function to use argparse. I'll keep the same logic but replace the argument parsing:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/ORNL-MDF__Myna__1.0/myna/core/workflow/launch_from_peregrine.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def launch_from_peregrine(argv=sys.argv):
    # Set up argument parser
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description="Launch Myna workflow from Peregrine",
        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter,
        epilog="""
Examples:
  myna launch_peregrine --build server/2024/08/example_build --parts [P5,P21] --layers [1,4,10] --mode gv
  myna launch_peregrine --build /path/to/build --parts "[P1,P2,P3]" --layers "[1,2,3,4,5]" --resolution 20e-6 --mode gv --workspace custom.myna-workspace
        """
    )
    
    parser.add_argument(
        "--build", 
        required=True,
        help="Path to the build directory"
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--parts", 
        required=True,
        help="List of parts to export, e.g., [P5,P21] or \"[P1,P2,P3]\""
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--layers", 
        required=True,
        help="List of layers to simulate, e.g., [1,4,10] or \"[1,2,3]\""
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--resolution", 
        type=float,
        help="Simulation resolution (overrides workspace file value)"
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--mode", 
        required=True,
        choices=['gv'],
        help="Simulation mode (currently only 'gv' is supported)"
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--workspace",
        help="Path to workspace file (default: peregrine_default.myna-workspace in cli/peregrine_launcher)"
    )
    
    # Parse arguments
    args = parser.parse_args(argv[1:])
    
    # Set working directory to the peregrine_launcher interface
    peregrine_launcher_path = os.path.join(
        os.environ["MYNA_INSTALL_PATH"], "cli", "peregrine_launcher"
    )
    with working_directory(peregrine_launcher_path):
        # Load workspace file if provided or use default
        workspace_data = {}
        workspace_path = args.workspace
        if workspace_path is None:
            # Use default workspace file
            workspace_path = os.path.join(peregrine_launcher_path, "peregrine_default.myna-workspace")
            
        if os.path.exists(workspace_path):
            try:
                workspace_data = load_input(workspace_path)
                print(f"Loaded workspace file: {workspace_path}")
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"Warning: Could not load workspace file {workspace_path}: {e}")
        else:
            print(f"Warning: Workspace file not found: {workspace_path}")
        
        # Extract parameters with workspace defaults
        build_path = args.build
        layers = [int(x) for x in myna.core.utils.str_to_list(args.layers)]
        exported_parts = myna.core.utils.str_to_list(args.parts)
        
        # Get resolution from command line or workspace
        resolution = args.resolution
        if resolution is None:
            # Try to get from workspace
            try:
                peregrine_config = workspace_data.get("peregrine", {})
                launcher_config = peregrine_config.get("launcher", {})
                resolution = launcher_config.get("resolution", 25e-6)  # Default fallback
                print(f"Using resolution from workspace: {resolution}")
            except:
                resolution = 25e-6  # Hard-coded fallback
                print(f"Using default resolution: {resolution}")
        else:
            print(f"Using resolution from command line: {resolution}")
            
        mode = args.mode.lower()

        # Write Peregrine input to log file
        lines = []
        lines.append(f"""argv = {argv[0]} {' '.join([f'"{x}"' for x in argv[1:]])}\n""")
        lines.append(f"{build_path=}\n")
        lines.append(f"{layers=}\n")
        lines.append(f"{exported_parts=}\n")
        lines.append(f"{resolution=}\n")
        lines.append(f"{mode=}\n")
        if args.workspace:
            lines.append(f"workspace={args.workspace}\n")

        # Get yyyy-mm-dd_hh-mm format for the current time and add to log file
        now = datetime.datetime.now()
        now_str_pretty = now.strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
        now_str_id = now.strftime("%Y-%m-%d-%Hh-%Mm")
        lines.append(f"\nSimulation started at {now_str_pretty}\n")

        # Set input file paths
        input_file = f"input_{mode}.yaml"
        output_basedir = "myna_output"
        input_file_configured = os.path.join(
            output_basedir, f"input_{mode}_{now_str_id}.yaml"
        )
        os.makedirs(output_basedir, exist_ok=True)

        # Get configurable key words
        config_file = "config.yaml"
        with open(config_file, "r") as f:
            config_dict = yaml.safe_load(f)

        # Set case-specific configurable key words
        config_dict["3DTHESIS_RESOLUTION"] = resolution
        config_dict["MYNA_INPUT_FILE"] = os.path.basename(input_file_configured)

        # Update key words
        marker = str(config_dict["MARKER"])
        with open(input_file, "r") as f:
            input_lines = f.readlines()
        for i in range(len(input_lines)):
            for key in config_dict.keys():
                if key != "MARKER":
                    old = marker + key + marker
                    new = str(config_dict[key])
                    old_line = input_lines[i]
                    new_line = input_lines[i].replace(old, new)
                    input_lines[i] = new_line
                    if old_line == new_line:
                        print(key)
                        print("\t" + old + " --> " + new)
                        print("\told: " + old_line)
                        print("\tnew: " + new_line)

        with open(input_file_configured, "w") as f:
            f.writelines(input_lines)

        # Read and update input dictionary
        output_dir = os.path.basename(build_path).replace(" ", "_") + f"_{now_str_id}"
        with open(input_file_configured, "r") as f:
            input_dict = yaml.safe_load(f)
        input_dict["data"] = {}
        input_dict["data"]["build"] = {}
        input_dict["data"]["build"]["name"] = output_dir
        input_dict["data"]["build"]["path"] = build_path
        input_dict["data"]["build"]["parts"] = {}
        for part in exported_parts:
            input_dict["data"]["build"]["parts"][part] = {"layers": layers}

        # Export updated input dictionary
        with open(input_file_configured, "w") as f:
            yaml.dump(input_dict, f, default_flow_style=False)

    # Set working directory to the peregrine_launcher interface output directory
    # to run all myna scripts
    with working_directory(os.path.join(peregrine_launcher_path, output_basedir)):
        # Construct myna_config command
        input_file_configured = os.path.basename(input_file_configured)
        cmd = f"myna_config --input {input_file_configured}"
        p = subprocess.run(
            cmd, shell=True, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT
        )
        out = p.stdout.decode()

        # Parse output
        lines.append(f"{cmd=}\n\n")
        for line in out.split("\r\n"):
            print(line)
            lines.append(line + "\n")
        lines.append("\n")

        # Construct myna_run command
        cmd = f"myna_run --input {input_file_configured}"
        p = subprocess.run(
            cmd, shell=True, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT
        )
        out = p.stdout.decode()

        # Parse output
        lines.append(f"{cmd=}\n\n")
        for line in out.split("\r\n"):
            print(line)
            lines.append(line + "\n")
        lines.append("\n")

        # Get yyyy-mm-dd_hh-mm format for the current time
        now = datetime.datetime.now()
        now_str_pretty = now.strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")

        # Add time to log file
        lines.append(f"\nSimulation completed at {now_str_pretty}\n")

        # Write log file
        with open(f"launch_from_peregrine_{now_str_id}.log", "w") as f:

    parser.add_argument(

        action="store",
        nargs="+",
        help='Run one or more stages of the myna workflow: "config", "run", "sync". Or output the current "status" or "launch_peregrine"',
    )

    args = parser.parse_args()
